### PART 4 — Certified material

#### 21. Requirements for certification as certified material

(1) Propagating material other than mother plants or a fruit plant may be officially certified as certified material if it has been found on official inspection and, where appropriate, through administrative checks, to fulfil the following requirements:—

- (a) it has been propagated from a certified mother plant;
- (b) the certified mother plant mentioned in head (a) has been grown from pre-basic material or basic material;
- (c) the certified mother plant mentioned in head (a) complies with the requirements in paragraph 24;
- (d) it complies with the requirements in—
  - (i) paragraph 7;
  - (ii) paragraph 8(5); and
  - (iii) paragraph 12;
- (e) it complies with the requirements in paragraph 23; and
- (f) it complies with the requirements in paragraph 24.

(2) For the purposes of sub-paragraph (1)(d), references in paragraphs 7, 8(5) and 12 to—

- (a) pre-basic mother plants, may be construed as references to certified mother plants; and
- (b) pre-basic material, may be construed as references to certified material.

#### 22. Rootstocks not belonging to a variety

(1) A rootstock which does not belong to a variety may be officially certified as certified material if it has been found on official inspection and, where appropriate, through administrative checks, to fulfil the following requirements:—

- (a) it is true to the description of its species;
- (b) it complies with the requirements in—
  - (i) paragraph 8(5); and
  - (ii) paragraph 12; and
- (c) it complies with the requirements in paragraphs 23 and 24.

(2) For the purposes of sub-paragraph (1)(b), references in paragraphs 8(5) and 12 to—

- (a) pre-basic mother plants, may be construed as references to certified mother plants; and
- (b) pre-basic material may, be construed as references to certified material.

#### 23. Health requirements for certified mother plants and certified material[^c-3cc95aef2c8fd339222de503320747d9]

(1) A certified mother plant or certified material must, by visual inspection in the facilities, fields and lots, be found free from the RNQPs listed in schedules 6 and 6A in accordance with the provisions of column 3 of schedule 7, as regards the genus or species concerned.

(2) The visual inspection referred to in sub-paragraph (1) must be carried out by or on behalf of the Scottish Ministers and, where appropriate, the supplier.

(3) The Scottish Ministers, persons acting on their behalf and, where appropriate, the supplier must carry out sampling and testing of the certified mother plant or certified material for the RNQPs listed in schedule 6A in accordance with the provisions of column 4 of schedule 7, with regard to the genus or species concerned and category.

(4) Where there are doubts concerning the presence of the RNQPs listed in schedule 6, the Scottish Ministers, persons acting on their behalf and, where appropriate, the supplier must carry out sampling and testing of the certified mother plant or certified material concerned.

(5) In relation to the sampling and testing referred to in sub-paragraphs (3) and (4)—

- (a) Scottish Ministers, persons acting on their behalf and the supplier must—
  - (i) apply protocols of EPPO, or other internationally recognised protocols, or
  - (ii) where the protocols referred to in sub-head (i) do not exist—
    - (aa) apply the relevant protocols established at national level, and
    - (bb)[^c-a97079dd25738d25c5d308f343ade4ef] . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . .
- (b) where appropriate, the supplier must submit samples to laboratories officially accepted by the Scottish Ministers.

(6) In the event of a positive test result for any of the RNQPs listed in schedules 6 and 6A, as regards the genus or species concerned, the supplier must remove the infested certified mother plant or certified material from the vicinity of other certified mother plants and certified material pursuant to regulation 15, or take appropriate measures pursuant to columns 4 and 5 of schedule 7.

(7) The measures to ensure compliance with the requirements of sub-paragraphs (1) to (4) are set out in schedule 7, with regard to the genus or species concerned and category.

(8) Sub-paragraphs (1) to (4) do not apply to certified mother plants and certified material during cryopreservation.

#### 24. Soil requirements for certified mother plants and certified material[^c-3690757d421bbc7c650d3fb7c6cfbf29]

(1) Subject to sub-paragraphs (2) and (3), certified mother plants must only be grown in soil which is found, on the basis of sampling and testing carried out prior to the planting of such mother plants, to be free from any pest listed in schedule 6B[^c-c83db63ef1af696dd3d4555590284a64] for the genus or species concerned and which may host viruses affecting that genus or species.

(2) Sampling and testing is not required under sub-paragraph (1) if—

- (a) there have been no plants which are hosts for any of the pests listed in schedule 6B grown in the relevant soil for a period of at least 5 years prior to the planting of the certified mother plants mentioned in sub-paragraph (1) and where there is no doubt concerning the absence of the relevant pests in the soil; or
- (b) an official inspection is carried out which concludes that the soil is free from any of the pests listed in schedule 6B for the genus or species concerned and which is host to viruses affecting that genus or species.

(2A)[^c-e71e7b727eb4aebd5c6a8c06de8f8d17] Unless otherwise stated, sampling and testing is not required under sub-paragraph (1) in the case of certified fruit plants.

(3) Sampling and testing of the soil must be carried out during the growing period of the certified mother plant mentioned in sub-paragraph (1) where there is suspicion concerning the presence of the pests mentioned in that sub-paragraph.

(4) In this paragraph, “**sampling and testing**” means sampling and testing carried out by or on behalf of the Scottish Ministers and, where appropriate, by the supplier—

- (a) in accordance with the appropriate EPPO protocol; and
- (b) taking into account the climatic conditions and the biology of the pests listed in schedule 6B and their relevance to the certified mother plants concerned.

### PART 5 — Requirements with regard to the production site, place of production or area[^c-f44e5144bc5c2c9451cb655e864bd6f7]

#### 25. Requirements with regard to the production site, place of production or area

In addition to the health and soil requirements of paragraphs 9, 10, 11, 17, 18, 23 and 24, propagating material and fruit plants must be produced in accordance with the requirements for the production site, place of production, or area as laid down in column 5 of schedule 7, in order to limit the presence of the RNQPs listed in that schedule for the genus or species concerned.

## SCHEDULE 3 — CAC material[^c-086c0d312df5d53de15b560a25038163]

Regulation 2(1)

### 1. Conditions for CAC material other than rootstocks not belonging to a variety

(1) CAC material other than rootstocks not belonging to a variety may only be marketed if it fulfils the following conditions—

- (a) it is propagated from an identified source of material recorded by the supplier;
- (b) its trueness to the description of its variety is established and verified in accordance with paragraph 3;
- (c) it is found by the supplier on visual inspection carried out in the facilities, fields and lots at the stage of production, to be substantially free from the RNQPs listed in schedules 6 and[^c-e14747b44251f81a89ddc2d42e0dffc6] 6A, as regards the genus or species concerned, unless stated otherwise in schedule 7;
- (ca)[^c-bc3f46762101901df9de5f142cdedc0d] in relation to the RNQPs listed in schedule 6A, sampling and testing by the supplier is carried out in accordance with column 4 of schedule 7 with regard to the genus or species concerned and category;
- (cb) in the event that there are doubts concerning the presence of the RNQPs listed in schedule 6, it is found as a result of sampling and testing by the supplier to be substantially free from those RNQPs;
- (d)[^c-4cee5aa9aa5b8dc567d3c14c2446916d] . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . .
- (e) on visual inspection, it is found to be practically free from defects including injuries, discolouration, scar tissue or dessication that affect the quality and usefulness of the plant as propagating material.

(1A)[^c-75a0207ff06f701c550cf58ad1f951ab] CAC propagating material and CAC fruit plants in lots, after the stage of production, may only be marketed if found free from signs or symptoms of the pests listed in schedules 6 and 6A, upon visual inspection carried out by the supplier.

(2) The visual inspections and sampling and testing mentioned in sub-paragraphs (1)(c), (ca) and (cb) and (1A)[^c-e143329f23ece59d07878e2b7d775d0a] may not be conducted during cryopreservation.

### 2. CAC material: rootstocks not belonging to a variety

(1) CAC material consisting of rootstocks not belonging to a variety may only be marketed if it fulfils the following conditions—

- (a) it is true to the description of its species;
- (b) it is found by the supplier on visual inspection, or where there is doubt, by sampling and testing, to be substantially free from the pests listed in schedules 6 and 6A[^c-6df731112a63f250d22e29d60a2af9c7]; and
- (c) on visual inspection, it is found to be substantially free from defects including injuries, discolouration, scar tissue or dessication that affect the quality and usefulness of the plant as propagating material.

(2) The visual inspections mentioned in sub-paragraph (1)(b) may not be conducted during cryopreservation.

### 3. CAC material: trueness to the description of the variety

(1) A supplier must establish and regularly verify the trueness of CAC material to the description of its variety in accordance with this paragraph.

(2) The trueness of certified material to the description of its variety must be established and verified by observation of the expression of the characteristics of the variety based on one of the following descriptions—

- (a) its official description;
- (b) the description accompanying the application to be a registered variety;
- (c) the description accompanying the application for a grant of plant variety rights; or
- (d) where the variety is registered with an officially recognised description, or subject to an application for registration with an officially recognised description, that description.

### 4. Requirements with regard to the production site, place of production or area[^c-589c31374608bc13bedfcd5545d19071]

In addition to the health and soil requirements of paragraph 1(1)(c), (ca) and (cb), (1A) and (2), propagating material and fruit plants must be produced in accordance with the requirements for the production site, place of production, or area as laid down in column 5 of schedule 7, in order to limit the presence of the RNQPs listed in that schedule for the genus or species concerned.

## SCHEDULE 4 — Registration of varieties

Regulations 2(1) and 7(3)

### 1. Interpretation

In this schedule—

- [^c-fc71f1f46bd66bdfae84bf74676a7d09]...
- [^c-fc71f1f46bd66bdfae84bf74676a7d09]...
- “distinctness” (and “distinct”) in relation to a variety, is to be construed in accordance with paragraph 9;
- “**the register**” means the register of varieties maintained by the Scottish Ministers under paragraph 2;
- “stability” (and “stable”) in relation to a variety, is to be construed in accordance with paragraph 11;
- “**technical questionnaire**” means the technical questionnaire set out in—
  - (a) any UPOV[^c-2fc8d01b925030da877e73e370d603f4] protocols published in relation to the relevant species; or
  - (b) where no such UPOV protocols have been published as mentioned in sub-paragraph (a), any UPOV guidelines published in relation to the relevant species; or
  - (c) where no such protocols as mentioned in sub-paragraph (a) or guidelines as mentioned in sub-paragraph (b) have been published, a protocol or guidelines established by or on behalf of the Scottish Ministers in relation to the same matters;
- “uniformity” (and “uniform”) in relation to a variety, is to be construed in accordance with paragraph 10;
- [^c-2a4454a6cdb77406f2a7100ab86d52d0]...
- “UPOV guidelines” mean test guidelines for the conduct of tests for distinctness, uniformity and stability that have been produced for the relevant species by the UPOV and which are applicable at the beginning of the growing trial [^c24593901].

### 2. Register of varieties

(1) The Scottish Ministers must maintain a register of varieties.

(2) The register must include—

- (a) the denomination of the variety and any synonyms;
- (b) the species to which the variety belongs;
- (c) the date of registration of the variety or, where applicable, the date of renewal of the registration; and
- (d) the date of the expiry of the validity of the registration.

(3) The register must state whether a variety has an official description or an officially recognised description.

(4) In relation to each variety registered, the Scottish Ministers must also keep a file containing a description of the variety and a summary of the facts relevant to its registration.

### 3. Registration

(1) The Scottish Ministers must register a variety with an official description if they are satisfied that—

- (a) the variety is distinct, uniform and stable;
- (b) a sample of the variety is available; and
- (c)[^c-903d94b849f423addeb8f5185579ba69] in relation to genetically modified varieties, the genetically modified organism of which the variety consists is authorised for cultivation pursuant to—
  - (i) the GMO regulations,
  - (ii) Regulation [(EC) No 1829/2003](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/eur/2003/1829), or
  - (iii) before the day on which IP completion day falls, Directive [2001/18/EC](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/eudr/2001/18).

(2) Before being satisfied in accordance with sub-paragraph (1)(a) that a variety is distinct, uniform and stable, the Scottish Ministers must take into account the results of growing trials in accordance with paragraph 5, carried out in respect of the variety by or on behalf of—

- (a) the Scottish Ministers; or
- (b)[^c-956dd34bf1540d643e1fa1c8c8983346] a responsible official body outside Scotland.

(3) The Scottish Ministers may register a variety that has been marketed within the European Union prior to 30th September 2012 with an officially recognised description.

### 4. Application for registration with an official description

(1) An application for registration of a variety with an official description must be made in writing to the Scottish Ministers in such form as the Scottish Ministers may require.

(2) An application must be accompanied by—

- (a) the information required in a technical questionnaire at the time of the application or, where no technical questionnaire is available in relation to the relevant species, such information as the Scottish Ministers may require;
- (b) information on whether the variety is officially registered in another country[^c-ea23b6629b2b802e13700dc08100612f], or is the subject of an application for such a registration;
- (c) a proposed denomination;
- (d)[^c-e065b0a7043e3ca643643a0f764666e5] in the case of a genetically modified variety, evidence that the genetically modified organism contained in that variety has been authorised for cultivation pursuant to—
  - (i) the GMO regulations,
  - (ii) Regulation [(EC) No 1829/2003](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/eur/2003/1829), or
  - (iii) before the day on which IP completion day falls, Directive [2001/18/EC](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/eudr/2001/18);
- (e) such other information as the Scottish Ministers may require.

(3) Where applicable, an application may be accompanied by details of an official description established by a responsible official body outside Scotland[^c-b45d1084ed0732957454b8db004ea741].

(4) Where applicable, an application may also be accompanied by a statement of any other relevant information.

(5) Where a growing trial is required, a sample of the material of the variety must be submitted on request.

### 5. Growing trials

(1) Growing trials must—

- (a) establish whether a variety is distinct, uniform and stable;
- (b) in relation to trial design, growing conditions and characteristics of the variety to be covered, be conducted in accordance with—
  - (i) UPOV[^c-144c1c30c304b7aca68cccdcb62d823c] protocols;
  - (ii) if no UPOV protocols have been published for the relevant species, any UPOV guidelines; or
  - (iii) if no UPOV protocols or UPOV guidelines have been published, any applicable protocols produced by, or on behalf of, the Scottish Ministers.

(2) Growing trials are not required if the Scottish Ministers are satisfied that an official description submitted in accordance with paragraph 4(3), or information submitted along with the official description, demonstrates that the variety is distinct, uniform and stable.

### 6. Duration and renewal of acceptance

(1) The registration of a variety with an official description or, as the case may be, the renewal of such a registration, is valid—

- (a) for the period up to the end of the 30th calendar year from the date of registration or renewal; or
- (b) in the case of genetically modified varieties, for the shorter of either—
  - (i) the period up to the end of the 30th calendar year from the date of registration or renewal; or
  - (ii)[^c-f5c7f0dfd6387ac7ca94b7a3288eada1] the period during which the genetically modified organism of which the variety consists is authorised for cultivation pursuant to—
    - (aa) the GMO regulations,
    - (bb) Regulation [(EC) No 1829/2003](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/eur/2003/1829), or
    - (cc) before the day on which IP completion day falls, Directive [2001/18/EC](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/eudr/2001/18).

(2) The Scottish Ministers may—

- (a) renew registration in accordance with sub-paragraph (3) or (4); or
- (b) revoke registration in accordance with paragraph 7.

(3) The Scottish Ministers may, following an application made in writing to them, renew the registration of a variety with an official description for a period described in sub-paragraph (1), provided that—

- (a) the requirements of distinctness, uniformity and stability are still satisfied in respect of the variety; and
- (b) there is still material of that variety available on the market.

(4) The Scottish Ministers may renew the registration in the absence of a written application where they are satisfied that renewal serves to preserve genetic diversity and sustainable production and the conditions mentioned in sub-paragraph (3) are met.

### 7. Removal from register

(1) Subject to sub-paragraph (2), the Scottish Ministers must revoke the registration of a variety if—

- (a) it is no longer distinct, uniform and stable;
- (b) there is no longer available any material of that variety that is sufficiently uniform or which corresponds to the description of the variety at the time of registration;
- (c) false or misleading information material to registration was provided to the Scottish Ministers in connection with the application for registration;
- (d)[^c-b84757606be49fca308159d04cbe98a7] in the case of any genetically modified variety, the genetically modified organism contained in that variety—
  - (i) ceases to be authorised for cultivation pursuant to—
    - (aa) the GMO regulations,
    - (bb) Regulation [(EC) No 1829/2003](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/eur/2003/1829), or
  - (ii) has, before the day on which IP completion day falls been authorised for cultivation pursuant to Directive [2001/18/EC](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/eudr/2001/18) and ceases to be authorised.

(2) But heads (a) to (c) of sub-paragraph (1) do not apply if the Scottish Ministers are satisfied that the variety should remain on the register in the interests of preserving the genetic diversity of varieties.

### 8. Additional requirements for products to be used as genetically modified food or feed

(1) This paragraph applies to any variety from which products are derived for use as, or in—

- (a) food within the scope of Article 3 of Regulation [(EC) No 1829/2003](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/european/regulation/2003/1829); or
- (b) feed within the scope of Article 15 of that Regulation.

(2) Before registering any such variety, the Scottish Ministers must be satisfied that the food or feed has been authorised pursuant to Regulation [(EC) No 1829/2003](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/european/regulation/2003/1829).

### 9. Distinctness

A variety is distinct if it is clearly distinguishable by one or more characteristics that result from a particular genotype or combination of genotypes, from any other variety whose existence is a matter of common knowledge at the time of the application for registration.

### 10. Uniformity

A variety is uniform if, subject to the variation that may be expected from the particular features of its propagation, it is sufficiently uniform in the expression of those characteristics which are included in the examination for distinctness, as well as any other characteristics used for the variety description.

### 11. Stability

A variety is stable if the expression of those characteristics which are included in the examination for distinctness, as well as any others used for the variety description, remain unchanged after repeated propagation or, in the case of micropropagation, at the end of each such cycle.

## SCHEDULE 5 — Official labels, supplier's documents and accompanying documents[^c-7f77d301901297ac5ace8c7ec6671a46]

Regulations 2(1), 5(1)(g), 8 and 9

### PART 1 — Official labels

**1.** An official label used in relation to pre-basic material must [^c-db7596babd36db4b80f72618f94ba861]... be white with a diagonal violet stripe.

**2.** An official label used in relation to basic material must be white.

**3.** An official label used in relation to certified material must [^c-a41265bb68c7f0ed9e58d28940ee7f2d]... be blue.

**4.** An official label must—

- (a) contain the statement “UK[^c-876e174228d9746a79f46aa97602308d] rules and standards”; and
- (b) state the following particulars:—
  - (i) the name of the responsible official body and country[^c-534de79d2531b18cefc1ca8bb3ca9557] or their distinguishing abbreviations;
  - (ii) the name of the supplier or their registration number;
  - (iii) the reference number of the package, container or bundle, the individual serial number, the week number or the batch number;
  - (iv) the botanical name of the plant material;
  - (v) the category of the plant material and, for basic material, the generation number;
  - (vi) the denomination of the variety and, where appropriate, the clone.
  - (vii) in the case of rootstocks not belonging to a variety, the name of the species or the interspecific hybrid concerned;
  - (viii) in the case of grafted fruit plants, the information in heads (vi) (in relation to the top-graft) and (vii) (in relation to the rootstock);
  - (ix) for varieties in respect of which an application to be a registered variety or for the grant of a plant variety right is pending, any information given in relation to heads (vi) to (viii) must be pre-fixed with the words “proposed denomination” and “application pending”;
  - (x) where appropriate, the words “variety with an officially recognised description”;
  - (xi) the quantity of plant material;
  - (xii) where different from the country[^c-a2203dabe310851a6a12aeeb67bc1723] of labelling, the country of production and its respective code or abbreviation;
  - (xiii) the year of issue, or in the case of a replacement label, the year of issue of the original label;
  - (xiv) in the case of a genetically modified variety—
    - (aa) a statement that the variety has been genetically modified; and
    - (bb) a list of the genetically modified organisms.

**5.** The information and particulars required on an official label must be indelibly printed in English, but may also be printed in other languages,[^c-7808b966746f55985c23099a34234adb] and must be easily visible and legible.

### PART 2 — Supplier's documents for CAC material

**6.** A supplier's document for CAC material must—

- (a) contain the statement “UK[^c-fb8eb98cca548d14290142b94003d74a] rules and Standards”; and
- (b) state the following particulars:—
  - (i) the name of the responsible official body and country[^c-29dfd25a23cbe7faa00b26f13fe23632] where the supplier's document was prepared or their distinguishing abbreviations;
  - (ii) the registration number of the supplier;
  - (iii) the individual serial, week number or batch number;
  - (iv) the botanical name of the plant material;
  - (v) CAC material;
  - (vi) the denomination of the variety or, in the case of rootstock, the denomination of the variety of its designation;
  - (vii)[^c-fd879c20768942c7c5d0e2e537d2791a] . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . .
  - (viii)[^c-bd872880628a4a61c70103429e711fe2] . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . .
  - (ix) the date of issue.

**6A.**[^c-ffa02ca9120dee99fcb112bc8a148fd1] Where it is affixed to the CAC material in the form of a label, the supplier’s document must be yellow.

**7.** The information and particulars required in a supplier's document must be indelibly printed in English, but may also be printed in other languages,[^c-420b2a6a47c56cdceeac56d988635a35] and must be clearly[^c-82587412721aa86ce045db8869c7a3a2] visible and legible.

### PART 3 — Accompanying documents

**8.** An accompanying document must—

- (a) contain the information listed in paragraph 4;
- (b) be written in English, but may also be written in other languages[^c-365caa462350bd122f2afb7bb8092c99];
- (c) be delivered at least in duplicate (supplier and recipient);
- (d) accompany the material from the place of the supplier to the place of the recipient;
- (e) include the name and address of the recipient;
- (f) include the date of issue of the document; and
- (g) include, where appropriate, additional information relevant to the lots concerned.

## SCHEDULE 7 — Visual inspections, sampling and testing per genus or species and category[^c-37ba69672913230bf66da5f60f049922]

Schedule 2, paragraphs 10(1),(3), (6) and (7), 17(1), (3), (6)and (7) and 23(1), (3), (6) and (7);and schedule 3, paragraphs 1(1) and 4

**1.** In this schedule, “Commission Implementing Decision 2017/925” means Commission Implementing Decision (EU) 2017/925 temporarily authorising certain Member States to certify pre-basic material of certain species of fruit plants, produced in the field under non-insect proof conditions, and repealing Implementing Decision (EU) 2017/167.

**2.** Propagating material must comply with—

- (a) the requirements concerning GB[^c-678e705deaa0287468a0c27669c58b4f] quarantine pests and protected zone quarantine pests provided for in implementing acts adopted pursuant to the EU Plant Health Regulation, as well as the measures adopted pursuant to Article 30(1) of that Regulation, and
- (b) the following requirements per genera or species and category concerned—

  <table>
  <thead>
  <tr><th><em><strong>Column 1</strong></em><br><em><strong>Genus or species</strong></em></th><th><em><strong>Column 2</strong></em><br><em><strong>Category</strong></em></th><th><em><strong>Column 3</strong></em><br><em><strong>Frequency of visual inspections</strong></em></th><th><em><strong>Column 4</strong></em><br><em><strong>Requirements relating to sampling and testing</strong></em></th><th><em><strong>Column 5</strong></em><br><em><strong>Requirements relating to the production site, place of production or area</strong></em></th></tr>
  </thead>
  <tbody>
  <tr><td rowspan="7"><em><strong>Castanea sativa</strong></em> <strong>Mill.</strong></td><td rowspan="2"><strong>Pre-basic category</strong></td><td rowspan="7">Visual inspections must be carried out once a year.</td><td rowspan="7">Sampling and testing must be carried out where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedule 6.</td><td>In the case where a derogation is allowed to produce pre-basic material in the field under non-insect proof conditions, pursuant to Commission Implementing Decision 2017/925, the following requirements apply concerning <em>Cryphonectria parasitica</em> (Murrill) Barr:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the pre-basic category must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Cryphonectria parasitica</em> (Murrill) Barr, or<br>(b) no symptoms of <em>Cryphonectria parasitica</em> (Murrill) Barr are observed at the site of production on propagating material and fruit plants of the pre-basic category since the beginning of the last complete cycle of vegetation.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="2"><strong>Basic category</strong></td><td>Propagating material and fruit plants of the basic category must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Cryphonectria parasitica</em> (Murrill) Barr, or</td></tr>
  <tr><td>no symptoms of <em>Cryphonectria parasitica</em> (Murrill) Barr are observed at the site of production on propagating material and fruit plants of the basic category since the beginning of the last complete cycle of vegetation.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="3"><strong>Certified and CAC categories</strong></td><td>Propagating material and fruit plants of the certified and CAC categories must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Cryphonectria parasitica</em> (Murrill) Barr,</td></tr>
  <tr><td>no symptoms of <em>Cryphonectria parasitica</em> (Murrill) Barr are observed at the site of production on propagating material and fruit plants of the certified and CAC categories since the beginning of the last complete cycle of vegetation, or</td></tr>
  <tr><td>propagating material and fruit plants of the certified and CAC categories showing symptoms of <em>Cryphonectria parasitica</em> (Murrill) Barr have been rogued out, the remaining propagating material and fruit plants must be inspected at weekly intervals and no symptoms are observed at the site of production for at least three weeks before dispatch.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="17"><em><strong>Citrus</strong></em> <strong>L.,</strong> <em><strong>Fortunella</strong></em> <strong>Swingle and</strong> <em><strong>Poncirus</strong></em> <strong>Raf.</strong></td><td rowspan="2"><strong>Pre-basic category</strong></td><td rowspan="2">Visual inspections must be carried out twice a year.</td><td>Each pre-basic mother plant must be sampled and tested every year concerning the presence of <em>Spiroplasma citri</em> Saglio <em>et al.</em> Each pre-basic mother plant must be sampled and tested three years after its acceptance as a pre-basic mother plant and with subsequent intervals of three years concerning the presence of <em>Citrus tristeza</em> virus (EU isolates).</td><td rowspan="2">N/A.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>Each pre-basic mother plant must be sampled and tested every six years after its acceptance as a pre-basic mother plant and with subsequent intervals of six years concerning the presence of RNQPs, other than <em>Citrus tristeza</em> virus (EU isolates) and <em>Spiroplasma citri</em> Saglio <em>et al.</em>, listed in schedule 6A, and where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedule 6.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="6"><strong>Basic category</strong></td><td rowspan="6">Visual inspections must be carried out twice a year with regard to <em>Citrus tristeza</em> virus (EU isolates), <em>Spiroplasma citri</em> Saglio <em>et al.</em> and <em>Plenodomus tracheiphilus</em> (Petri) Gruyter, Aveskamp &amp; Verkley. Visual inspections must be carried out once a year for all RNQPs, other than <em>Citrus tristeza</em> virus (EU isolates), <em>Spiroplasma citri</em> Saglio <em>et al.</em> and <em>Plenodomus tracheiphilus</em> (Petri) Gruyter, Aveskamp &amp; Verkley, listed in schedules 6 and 6A</td><td>In the case of basic mother plants which have been maintained in insect proof facilities, each basic mother plant must be sampled and tested every three years concerning the presence of <em>Citrus tristeza</em> virus (EU isolates). A representative portion of basic mother plants must be sampled and tested every three years concerning the presence of <em>Spiroplasma citri</em> Saglio <em>et al</em>.</td><td>Propagating material and fruit plants of the basic and certified categories must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Citrus tristeza</em> virus (EU isolates), <em>Spiroplasma citri</em> Saglio <em>et al.</em> and <em>Plenodomus tracheiphilus</em> (Petri) Gruyter, Aveskamp &amp; Verkley,</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="5">In the case of basic mother plants which have not been maintained in insect proof facilities, a representative portion of basic mother plants must be sampled and tested every year concerning the presence of <em>Citrus tristeza</em> virus (EU isolates) and <em>Spiroplasma citri Saglio et al.</em> in order to have all mother plants tested within an interval of 2 years. In the case of a positive test result for <em>Citrus tristeza</em> virus (EU isolates) all basic mother plants in the production site must be sampled and tested. A representative portion of basic mother plants which have not been maintained in insect proof facilities must be sampled and tested every six years on the basis of an assessment of the risk of infection of those plants concerning the presence of RNQPs, other than <em>Citrus tristeza</em> virus (EU isolates) and <em>Spiroplasma citri</em> Saglio <em>et al.</em>, listed in schedules 6 and 6A.</td><td>in the case of propagating material and fruit plants of the basic and certified categories which have been grown in insect proof facilities, no symptoms of <em>Spiroplasma citri</em> Saglio <em>et al.</em> or <em>Plenodomus tracheiphilus</em> (Petri) Gruyter, Aveskamp &amp; Verkley are observed on that propagating material and those fruit plants over the last complete growing season and the material has been subjected to random sampling and testing <em>Citrus tristeza</em> virus (EU isolates) before marketing,</td></tr>
  <tr><td>in the case of propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category which have not been grown in insect proof facilities, no symptoms of <em>Spiroplasma citri</em> Saglio <em>et al.</em> or <em>Plenodomus tracheiphilus (</em>Petri) Gruyter, Aveskamp &amp; Verkley are observed on that propagating material and those fruit plants over the last complete growing season, and a representative portion of the material has been sampled and tested for <em>Citrus tristeza</em> virus (EU isolates) before marketing, or</td></tr>
  <tr><td>in the case of propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category which have not been grown in insect proof facilities:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) symptoms of <em>Plenodomus tracheiphilus (</em>Petri) Gruyter, Aveskamp &amp; Verkley or <em>Spiroplasma citri</em> Saglio <em>et al.</em> are observed on no more than 2% of propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and that propagating material and those fruit plants, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed, and</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="4">(a) a representative portion of propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category has been sampled and tested for <em>Citrus tristeza</em> virus (EU isolates), before marketing and no more than 2% of propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category in the production site have been found positive over the last complete growing season. That propagating material and those fruit plants have been rogued out and immediately destroyed. Propagating material and fruit plants in the immediate vicinity have been subjected to random sampling and testing, and any propagating material and fruit plants which have been found positive have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="3"><strong>Certified category</strong></td><td rowspan="3">Visual inspections must be carried out twice a year with regard to <em>Citrus tristeza</em> virus (EU isolates), <em>Spiroplasma citri</em> Saglio <em>et al.</em> and <em>Plenodomus tracheiphilus</em> (Petri) Gruyter, Aveskamp &amp; Verkley. Visual inspections must be carried out once a year for all RNQPs, other than <em>Citrus tristeza</em> virus (EU isolates), <em>Spiroplasma citri</em> Saglio <em>et al.</em> and <em>Plenodomus tracheiphilus</em> (Petri) Gruyter, Aveskamp &amp; Verkley , listed in schedules 6 and 6A.</td><td>In the case of certified mother plants which have been maintained in insect proof facilities, a representative portion of certified mother plants must be sampled and tested every four years concerning the presence of <em>Citrus tristeza</em> virus (EU isolates) in order to have all mother plants tested within an interval of 8 years.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>In the case of certified mother plants which have not been maintained in insect proof facilities, a representative portion of certified mother plants must be sampled and tested every year concerning the presence of <em>Citrus tristeza</em> virus (EU isolates) in order to have all mother plants tested within an interval of 3 years. A representative portion of certified mother plants which have not been maintained in insect proof facilities must be sampled and tested where there are doubts concerning the presence of pests, other than <em>Citrus tristeza</em> virus (EU isolates), listed in schedules 6 and 6A.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>In the case of a positive test result for <em>Citrus tristeza</em> virus (EU isolates) all certified mother plants in the production site must be sampled and tested.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="6"><strong>CAC category</strong></td><td rowspan="6">Visual inspections must be carried out once a year.</td><td>Propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category must derive from an identified source of material, which has been found free, on the basis of visual inspection, sampling and testing, from the RNQPs as listed in schedule 6A.</td><td>Propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Citrus tristeza</em> virus (EU isolates), <em>Spiroplasma citri</em> Saglio <em>et al.</em> and <em>Plenodomus tracheiphilus</em> (Petri) Gruyter, Aveskamp &amp; Verkley,</td></tr>
  <tr><td>In the case the identified source of material has been maintained in insect proof facilities, a representative portion of that material must be sampled and tested every eight years concerning the presence of <em>Citrus tristeza</em> virus (EU isolates).</td><td rowspan="2">in the case of propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category which have been grown in insect proof facilities, no symptoms of <em>Spiroplasma citri</em> Saglio <em>et al.</em> or <em>Plenodomus tracheiphilus</em> (Petri) Gruyter, Aveskamp &amp; Verkley are observed on that propagating material and those fruit plants over the last complete growing season and the material has been subjected to random sampling and testing for <em>Citrus tristeza</em> virus (EU isolates) before marketing,</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="4">In the case the identified source of material has not been maintained in insect-proof facilities, a representative portion of that material must be sampled and tested every three years concerning the presence of <em>Citrus tristeza</em> virus (EU isolates).</td></tr>
  <tr><td>in the case of propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category which have not been grown in insect proof facilities, no symptoms of <em>Spiroplasma citri</em> Saglio <em>et al.</em> or <em>Plenodomus tracheiphilus</em> (Petri) Gruyter, Aveskamp &amp; Verkley are observed on propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed, and a representative portion of the material has been sampled and tested for <em>Citrus tristeza</em> virus (EU isolates) before marketing, or</td></tr>
  <tr><td>in the case of propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category which have not been grown in insect proof facilities:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) symptoms of <em>Spiroplasma citri</em> Saglio <em>et al.</em> or <em>Plenodomus tracheiphilus</em> (Petri) Gruyter, Aveskamp &amp; Verkley are observed on no more than 2% of propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and that propagating material and those fruit plants and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed, and<br>(b) a representative portion of propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category has been sampled and tested for <em>Citrus tristeza</em> virus (EU isolates), before marketing and no more than 2% of propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category in the production site have been found positive over the last complete growing season. That propagating material and those fruit plants have been rogued out and immediately destroyed. Propagating material and fruit plants in the immediate vicinity have been subjected to random sampling and testing, and any propagating material and fruit plants which have been found positive have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td><em><strong>Corylus avellana</strong></em> <strong>L.</strong></td><td><strong>All categories</strong></td><td>Visual inspections must be carried out once a year.</td><td>Sampling and testing must be carried out where there are doubts concerning the presence of the RNQPs listed in schedules 6 and 6A.</td><td>N/A.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="7"><em><strong>Cydonia oblonga</strong></em> <strong>Mill.</strong></td><td><strong>Pre-basic category</strong></td><td rowspan="7">Visual inspections must be carried out over the last complete growing season for <em>Erwinia amylovora</em> (Burrill) Winslow <em>et al.</em> For all RNQPs, other than E<em>rwinia amylovora</em> (Burrill) Winslow <em>et al.</em>, visual inspections must be carried out once a year.</td><td>Each pre-basic mother plant must be sampled and tested fifteen years after its acceptance as a pre-basic mother plant and with subsequent intervals of fifteen years concerning the presence of RNQPs other than virus-like diseases and viroids listed in schedule 6A, and where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedule 6.</td><td>N/A</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="2"><strong>Basic category</strong></td><td rowspan="2">A representative portion of basic mother plants must be sampled and tested every fifteen years on the basis of an assessment of the risk of infection of those plants concerning the presence of RNQPs other than virus-like diseases and viroids listed in schedule 6A, and where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedule 6.</td><td>Propagating material and fruit plants of the basic and certified categories must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Erwinia amylovora</em> (Burrill) Winslow <em>et al.</em>, or</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="3">propagating material and fruit plants of the basic and certified categories in the production site have been inspected over the last complete growing season, and any propagating material and fruit plants showing symptoms of <em>Erwinia amylovora</em> (Burrill) Winslow <em>et al.</em> and any surrounding host plants have been immediately rogued out and destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="2"><strong>Certified category</strong></td><td>A representative portion of certified mother plants must be sampled and tested every fifteen years on the basis of an assessment of the risk of infection of those plants concerning the presence of RNQPs other than virus-like diseases and viroids listed in schedule 6A, and where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedule 6.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>Certified fruit plants must be sampled and tested where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedules 6 and 6A.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="2"><strong>CAC category</strong></td><td rowspan="2">Sampling and testing must be carried out where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedules 6 and 6A.</td><td>Propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Erwinia amylovora</em> (Burrill) Winslow <em>et al.</em>, or</td></tr>
  <tr><td>propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category in the production site have been inspected over the last complete growing season, and any propagating material and fruit plants showing symptoms of <em>Erwinia amylovora</em> (Burrill) Winslow <em>et al.</em> and any surrounding host plants have been immediately rogued out and destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td><em><strong>Ficus carica</strong></em> <strong>L.</strong></td><td><strong>All categories</strong></td><td>Visual inspections must be carried out once a year.</td><td>Sampling and testing must be carried out where there are doubts concerning the presence of the RNQPs listed in schedule 6.</td><td>N/A.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="31"><em><strong>Fragaria</strong></em> <strong>L.</strong></td><td rowspan="2"><strong>Pre-basic category</strong></td><td>Visual inspections must be carried out twice a year during the growing season. The foliage of <em>Fragaria</em> L. must be visually inspected concerning the presence of <em>Phytophthora fragariae</em> C.J. Hickman.</td><td rowspan="2">Each pre-basic mother plant must be sampled and tested one year after its acceptance as a pre-basic mother plant and subsequently once per growing season concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedule 6A, and, where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedule 6.</td><td rowspan="2">N/A.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="13">For propagating material and fruit plants produced by micropropagation, and which are maintained for a period shorter than three months, only one visual inspection during this period is necessary.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="12"><strong>Basic category</strong></td><td rowspan="12">A representative sample of roots must be sampled and tested in the case of symptoms of <em>Phytophthora fragariae</em> C.J. Hickman on the foliage. Sampling and testing must be carried out if the symptoms of <em>Arabis</em> mosaic virus, Raspberry ringspot virus, Strawberry crinkle virus, Strawberry latent ringspot virus, Strawberry mild yellow edge virus, Strawberry vein banding virus and Tomato black ring virus are unclear upon visual inspection. Sampling and testing must be carried out where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs, other than <em>Arabis</em> mosaic virus, <em>Phytophthora fragariae</em> C.J. Hickman, Raspberry ringspot virus, Strawberry crinkle virus, Strawberry latent ringspot virus, Strawberry mild yellow edge virus, Strawberry vein banding virus, and Tomato black ring virus, listed in schedules 6 and 6A.</td><td><em>Phytophthora fragariae</em> C.J. Hickman:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the basic category must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Phytophthora fragariae</em> C.J. Hickman, or<br>(b) no symptoms of <em>Phytophthora fragariae</em> C.J. Hickman are observed on the foliage of propagating material and fruit plants of the basic category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any infected propagating material and fruit plants and plants in a surrounding zone of at least 5 m radius have been marked, excluded from lifting and marketing, and destroyed after uninfected propagating material and fruit plants have been lifted.</td></tr>
  <tr><td><em>Xanthomonas fragariae</em> Kennedy &amp; King:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the basic category must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Xanthomonas fragariae</em> Kennedy &amp; King, or<br>(b) no symptoms of <em>Xanthomonas fragariae</em> Kennedy &amp; King are observed on propagating material and fruit plants of the basic category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td><em>Phytophthora fragariae</em> C.J. Hickman:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) there must be a rest period, during which the propagating material and fruit plants concerned must not be grown, which must be of at least ten years between findings of <em>Phytophthora fragariae</em> C.J. Hickman and the next planting, or<br>(b) the cropping and soil borne disease history of the production site must be recorded.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>There must be a rest period, during which the propagating material and fruit plants concerned must not be grown, which must be of at least one year between findings of <em>Xanthomonas fragariae</em> Kennedy &amp; King and the next planting.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>Requirements for RNQPs, other than <em>Xanthomonas fragariae</em> Kennedy &amp; King and <em>Phytophthora fragariae</em> C.J. Hickman and other than viruses:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) the percentage of propagating material and fruit plants of the basic category in the production site over the last complete growing season, showing symptoms of each of the following RNQPs must not exceed:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(i) 0.05% in the case of <em>Aphelenchoides besseyi</em>,<br>(ii) 0.1% in the case of Strawberry multiplier disease phytoplasma,<br>(iii) 0.2% in the case of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma asteris Lee <em>et al</em>., <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma pruni, <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma solani Quaglino <em>et al</em>., <em>Verticillium albo-atrum</em> Reinke &amp; Berthold and <em>Verticillium dahliae</em> Kleb,<br>(iv) 0.5% in the case of <em>Chaetosiphon fragaefolii</em> Cockerell, <em>Ditylenchus dipsaci</em> (Kuehn) Filipjev, <em>Meloidogyne hapla</em> Chitwood, <em>Podosphaera aphanis</em> (Wallroth) Braun &amp; Takamatsu,<br>(v) 1% in the case of <em>Pratylenchus vulnus</em> Allen &amp; Jensen; and that propagating material and those fruit plants and any surrounding host plants have been rogued out and destroyed, and</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) in the case of a positive test result for propagating material and fruit plants of the basic category showing symptoms of <em>Arabis</em> mosaic virus, Raspberry ringspot virus, Strawberry crinkle virus, Strawberry latent ringspot virus, Strawberry mild yellow edge virus, Strawberry vein banding virus, and Tomato black ring virus, the propagating material and fruit plants concerned must be rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>Symptoms of all viruses listed in schedules 6 and 6A must have been observed on no more than 1% of propagating material and fruit plants of the basic category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and that propagating material and those fruit plants and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity must have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="12"><strong>Certified category</strong></td><td rowspan="17"></td><td rowspan="12">A representative sample of roots must be sampled and tested in the case of symptoms of <em>Phytophthora fragariae</em> C.J. Hickman on the foliage. Sampling and testing must be carried out if the symptoms of <em>Arabis</em> mosaic virus, Raspberry ringspot virus, Strawberry crinkle virus, Strawberry latent ringspot virus, Strawberry mild yellow edge virus, Strawberry vein banding virus, and Tomato black ring virus are unclear upon visual inspection. Sampling and testing must be carried out where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs, other than <em>Arabis</em> mosaic virus, <em>Phytophthora fragariae</em> C.J. Hickman, Raspberry ringspot virus, Strawberry crinkle virus, Strawberry latent ringspot virus, Strawberry mild yellow edge virus, Strawberry vein banding virus, and Tomato black ring virus, listed in schedules 6 and 6A.</td><td><em>Phytophthora fragariae</em> C.J. Hickman:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Phytophthora fragariae</em> C.J. Hickman, or<br>(b) no symptoms of <em>Phytophthora fragariae</em> C.J. Hickman are observed on the foliage of propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any infected propagating material and fruit plants and plants in a surrounding zone of at least 5m radius have been marked, excluded from lifting and marketing, and destroyed after uninfected plants have been lifted.</td></tr>
  <tr><td><em>Xanthomonas fragariae</em> Kennedy &amp; King:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Xanthomonas fragariae</em> Kennedy &amp; King, or<br>(b) symptoms of <em>Xanthomonas fragariae</em> Kennedy &amp; King have been observed on no more than 2% of propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and that propagating material and those fruit plants, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td><em>Phytophthora fragariae</em> C.J. Hickman:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) there must be a rest period, during which the propagating material and fruit plants concerned must not be grown, which must be of at least ten years between findings of <em>Phytophthora fragariae</em> C.J. Hickman and the next planting, or<br>(b) the cropping and soil borne disease history of the production site must be recorded.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>There must be a rest period, during which the propagating material and fruit plants concerned must not be grown, which must be of at least one year between findings of <em>Xanthomonas fragariae</em> Kennedy &amp; King and the next planting.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>Requirements for RNQPs, other than <em>Xanthomonas fragariae</em> Kennedy &amp; King and <em>Phytophthora fragariae</em> C.J. Hickman and other than viruses:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) the percentage of propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category in the production site over the last complete growing season, showing symptoms of each of the following RNQPs must not exceed:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(i) 0.1% in the case of <em>Phytonemus pallidus</em> Banks,<br>(ii) 0.5% in the case of <em>Aphelenchoides besseyi</em> Christie and Strawberry multiplier disease phytoplasma,<br>(iii) 1% in the case of <em>Aphelenchoides fragariae</em> (Ritzema Bos) Christie, <em>Candidatus</em> Phlomobacter fragariae Zreik, Bové &amp; Garnier, <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma asteris Lee <em>et al</em>., ... <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma fragariae Valiunas, Staniulis &amp; Davis, <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma pruni, <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma solani Quaglino <em>et al</em>., <em>Chaetosiphon fragaefolii</em> Cockerell, Clover phyllody phytoplasma, <em>Ditylenchus dipsaci</em> (Kuehn) Filipje, <em>Meloidogyne hapla</em> Chitwood, <em>Podosphaera aphanis</em> (Wallroth) Braun &amp; Takamatsu, <em>Pratylenchus vulnus</em> Allen &amp; Jensen and <em>Rhizoctonia fragariae</em> Hussain &amp; W.E.McKeen,<br>(iv) 2% in the case of <em>Verticillium albo-atrum</em> Reinke &amp; Berthold and <em>Verticillium dahliae</em> Kleb; and that propagating material and those fruit plants, and any surrounding host plants have been rogued out and destroyed, and</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) in the case of a positive test result for propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category showing symptoms of <em>Arabis</em> mosaic virus, Raspberry ringspot virus, Strawberry crinkle virus, Strawberry latent ringspot virus, Strawberry mild yellow edge virus, Strawberry vein banding virus, and Tomato black ring virus, the propagating material and fruit plants concerned must be rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>Symptoms of all viruses listed in schedules 6 and 6A have been observed on no more than 2% of propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and that propagating material and those fruit plants and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="5"><strong>CAC category</strong></td><td rowspan="5">A representative sample of roots must be sampled and tested in the case of symptoms of <em>Phytophthora fragariae</em> C.J. Hickman on the foliage. Sampling and testing must be carried out if the symptoms of <em>Arabis</em> mosaic virus, Raspberry ringspot virus, Strawberry crinkle virus, Strawberry latent ringspot virus, Strawberry mild yellow edge virus , Strawberry vein banding virus, and Tomato black ring virus are unclear upon visual inspection. Sampling and testing must be carried out where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs, other than <em>Arabis</em> mosaic virus, <em>Phytophthora fragariae</em> C.J. Hickman, Raspberry ringspot virus, Strawberry crinkle virus, Strawberry latent ringspot virus, Strawberry mild yellow edge virus, Strawberry vein banding virus, and Tomato black ring virus, listed in schedules 6 and 6A.</td><td><em>Phytophthora fragariae</em> C.J. Hickman:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Phytophthora fragariae</em> C.J. Hickman, or<br>(b) no symptoms of <em>Phytophthora fragariae</em> C.J. Hickman are observed on the foliage of propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any infected propagating material and fruit plants and plants in a surrounding zone of at least 5m radius have been marked, excluded from lifting and marketing, and destroyed after uninfected propagating material and fruit plants have been lifted.</td></tr>
  <tr><td><em>Xanthomonas fragariae</em> Kennedy &amp; King:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Xanthomonas fragariae</em> Kennedy &amp; King,<br>(b) no symptoms of <em>Xanthomonas fragariae</em> Kennedy &amp; King are observed on propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out, or<br>(c) symptoms of <em>Xanthomonas fragariae</em> Kennedy &amp; King have been observed on no more than 5% of propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and that propagating material and those fruit plants, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>In the case of a positive test result for propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category showing symptoms of <em>Arabis</em> mosaic virus, Raspberry ringspot virus, Strawberry crinkle virus, Strawberry latent ringspot virus, Strawberry mild yellow edge virus, Strawberry vein banding virus, and Tomato black ring virus, the propagating material and fruit plants concerned must be rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="5"><em><strong>Juglans regia</strong></em> <strong>L.</strong></td><td><strong>Pre-basic category</strong></td><td rowspan="5">Visual inspections must be carried out once a year.</td><td>Each flowering pre-basic mother plant must be sampled and tested one year after its acceptance as a pre-basic mother plant and with subsequent intervals of one year concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedule 6A, and where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedule 6.</td><td rowspan="5">N/A.</td></tr>
  <tr><td><strong>Basic category</strong></td><td>A representative portion of basic mother plants must be sampled and tested every year on the basis of an assessment of the risk of infection of those plants concerning the presence of the RNQPs listed in schedules 6 and 6A.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="2"><strong>Certified category</strong></td><td>A representative portion of certified mother plants must be sampled and tested every three years on the basis of an assessment of the risk of infection of those plants concerning the presence of the RNQPs listed in schedules 6 and 6A.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>Certified fruit plants must be sampled and tested where there are doubts concerning the presence of the RNQPs listed in schedules 6 and 6A.</td></tr>
  <tr><td><strong>CAC category</strong></td><td>Sampling and testing must be carried out where there are doubts concerning the presence of the RNQPs listed in schedules 6 and 6A.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="26"><em><strong>Malus</strong></em> <strong>Mill.</strong></td><td rowspan="7"><strong>Pre-basic category</strong></td><td rowspan="7">Visual inspections must be carried out once a year.</td><td rowspan="7">Each pre-basic mother plant must be sampled and tested fifteen years after its acceptance as a pre-basic mother plant and with subsequent intervals of fifteen years concerning the presence of RNQPs other than virus-like diseases and viroids listed in schedule 6A, and where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedule 6.</td><td>In the case where a derogation is allowed to produce pre-basic material in the field under non-insect proof conditions, pursuant to Commission Implementing Decision 2017/925, the following requirements apply concerning <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma mali Seemüller &amp; Schneider , <em>Erwinia amylovora</em> (Burrill) Winslow <em>et al.</em> and Tomato ringspot virus:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma mali Seemüller &amp; Schneider:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(i) propagating material and fruit plants of the pre-basic category must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma mali Seemüller &amp; Schneider, or<br>(ii) no symptoms of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma mali Seemüller &amp; Schneider are observed on propagating material and fruit plants of the pre-basic category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed,</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) <em>Erwinia amylovora</em> (Burrill) Winslow et al.:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(i) propagating material and fruit plants of the pre-basic category must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Erwinia amylovora</em> (Burrill) Winslow <em>et al.</em>, or<br>(ii) propagating material and fruit plants of the pre-basic category in the production site have been inspected over the last complete growing season, and any propagating material and fruit plants showing symptoms of <em>Erwinia amylovora</em> (Burrill) Winslow <em>et al.</em> and any surrounding host plants have been immediately rogued out and destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(c) Tomato ringspot virus:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(i) propagating material and fruit plants of the pre-basic category must be produced in areas known to be free from Tomato ringspot virus, or<br>(ii) no symptoms of Tomato ringspot virus are observed on propagating material and fruit plants of the pre-basic category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="6"><strong>Basic category</strong></td><td rowspan="19"></td><td rowspan="2"></td><td><em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma mali Seemüller &amp; Schneider:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the basic and certified categories must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma mali Seemüller &amp; Schneider,</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="4">In the case of basic mother plants which have not been maintained in insect-proof facilities, a representative portion of basic mother plants must be sampled and tested:<br>(a) every three years concerning the presence of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma mali Seemüller &amp; Schneider,<br>(b) every twenty years concerning the presence of Tomato ringspot virus,<br>(c) every fifteen years on the basis of an assessment of the risk of infection of those plants concerning the presence of RNQPs, other than <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma mali Seemüller &amp; Schneider, Tomato ringspot virus and virus-like diseases and viroids, listed in schedule 6A, and where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedule 6.</td><td>(a) no symptoms of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma mali Seemüller &amp; Schneider are observed on propagating material and fruit plants of the basic and certified categories in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed, or</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) symptoms of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma mali Seemüller &amp; Schneider have been observed on no more than 2% of propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and that propagating material and those fruit plants, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed, and a representative sample of the remaining asymptomatic propagating material and fruit plants in the lots in which symptomatic propagating material and fruit plants were found has been tested and found free from <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma mali Seemüller &amp; Schneider.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>Tomato ringspot virus:<br>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the basic category must be produced in areas known to be free from Tomato ringspot virus, or<br>(b) no symptoms of Tomato ringspot virus are observed on propagating material and fruit plants of the basic category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="2"></td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="5"><strong>Certified category</strong></td><td>In the case of certified mother plants which have not been maintained in insect-proof facilities, a representative portion of certified mother plants must be sampled and tested:<br>(a) every five years concerning the presence of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma mali Seemüller &amp; Schneider,<br>(b) every twenty years concerning the presence of Tomato ringspot virus,<br>(c) every fifteen years on the basis of an assessment of the risk of infection of those plants concerning the presence of RNQPs, other than <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma mali Seemüller &amp; Schneider, Tomato ringspot virus and virus-like diseases and viroids, listed in schedule 6A, and where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedule 6.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="4">Certified fruit plants must be sampled and tested where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedules 6 and 6A.</td><td><em>Erwinia amylovora</em> (Burrill) Winslow et al.:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the basic and certified categories must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Erwinia amylovora</em> (Burrill) Winslow <em>et al</em>., or</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the basic and certified categories in the production site have been inspected over the last complete growing season, and any propagating material and fruit plants showing symptoms of <em>Erwinia amylovora</em> (Burrill) Winslow <em>et al</em>. and any surrounding host plants have been immediately rogued out and destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>Tomato ringspot virus:<br>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category must be produced in areas known to be free from Tomato ringspot virus, or<br>(b) no symptoms of Tomato ringspot virus are observed on propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="8"><strong>CAC category</strong></td><td rowspan="8">Sampling and testing must be carried out where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedules 6 and 6A.</td><td><em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma mali <em>Seemüller &amp; Schneider</em>:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma mali Seemüller &amp; Schneider,</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) no symptoms of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma mali Seemüller &amp; Schneider are observed on propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed, or</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) symptoms of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma mali Seemüller &amp; Schneider have been observed on no more than 2% of propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed, and a representative sample of the remaining asymptomatic propagating material and fruit plants in the lots in which symptomatic propagating material and fruit plants were found has been tested and found free from <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma mali Seemüller &amp; Schneider.</td></tr>
  <tr><td><em>Erwinia amylovora</em> (Burrill) Winslow <em>et al</em>.:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Erwinia amylovora</em> (Burrill) Winslow <em>et al</em>., or</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category in the production site have been inspected over the last complete growing season, and any propagating material and fruit plants showing symptoms of <em>Erwinia amylovora</em> (Burrill) Winslow <em>et al</em>. and any surrounding host plants have been immediately rogued out and destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>Tomato ringspot virus:<br>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category must be produced in areas known to be free from Tomato ringspot virus,<br>(b) no symptoms of Tomato ringspot virus are observed on propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed, or<br>(c) symptoms of Tomato ringspot virus have been observed on no more than 2% of propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and those symptomatic plants have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="4"><em><strong>Olea europaea</strong></em> <strong>L.</strong></td><td><strong>Pre-basic category</strong></td><td rowspan="4">Visual inspections must be carried out once a year.</td><td>Each pre-basic mother plant must be sampled and tested ten years after its acceptance as a pre-basic mother plant and with subsequent intervals of ten years concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedule 6A, and where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedule 6.</td><td rowspan="4">N/A.</td></tr>
  <tr><td><strong>Basic category</strong></td><td>A representative portion of basic mother plants must be sampled in order to have all plants tested within an interval of thirty years on the basis of an assessment of the risk of infection of those plants concerning the presence of the RNQPs listed in schedules 6 and 6A.</td></tr>
  <tr><td><strong>Certified category</strong></td><td>In the case of mother plants used for the production of seeds (“seed mother plants”), a representative portion of those seed mother plants must be sampled in order to have all plants tested within an interval of forty years on the basis of an assessment of the risk of infection of those plants concerning the presence of the RNQPs listed in schedules 6 and 6A. In the case of mother plants other than seed mother plants, a representative portion of those plants must be sampled in order to have all plants tested within an interval of thirty years on the basis of an assessment of the risk of infection of those plants concerning the presence of the RNQPs listed in schedules 6 and 6A.</td></tr>
  <tr><td><strong>CAC category</strong></td><td>Sampling and testing must be carried out where there are doubts concerning the presence of the RNQPs listed in schedules 6 and 6A.</td></tr>
  <tr><td><em><strong>Pistacia vera</strong></em> <strong>L.</strong></td><td><strong>All categories</strong></td><td>Visual inspections must be carried out once a year.</td><td>Sampling and testing must be carried out where there are doubts concerning the presence of the RNQPs listed in schedule 6.</td><td>N/A.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="53"><em><strong>Prunus armeniaca</strong></em> <strong>L.,</strong> <em><strong>Prunus avium</strong></em> <strong>L.,</strong> <em><strong>Prunus cerasifera</strong></em> <strong>Ehrh.,</strong> <em><strong>Prunus cerasus</strong></em> <strong>L.,</strong> <em><strong>Prunus domestica</strong></em> <strong>L.,</strong> <em><strong>Prunus dulcis</strong></em> <strong>(Miller) Webb,</strong> <em><strong>Prunus persica</strong></em> <strong>(L.) Batsch and</strong> <em><strong>Prunus salicina</strong></em> <strong>Lindley</strong></td><td rowspan="15"><strong>Pre-basic category</strong></td><td rowspan="15">Visual inspections must be carried out twice a year with regard to <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider, Plum pox virus, <em>Xanthomonas arboricola</em> pv. <em>pruni</em> (Smith) Vauterin <em>et al.</em> and <em>Pseudomonas syringae</em> pv<em>. persicae</em> (Prunier, Luisetti &amp;. Gardan) Young, Dye &amp; Wilkie (<em>Prunus persica</em> (L.) Batsch and <em>Prunus salicina</em> Lindley). Visual inspections must be carried out once a year for all RNQPs, other than <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider, Plum pox virus, <em>Xanthomonas arboricola</em> pv. <em>pruni</em> (Smith) Vauterin <em>et al.</em> and <em>Pseudomonas syringae</em> pv<em>. persicae</em> (Prunier, Luisetti &amp;. Gardan) Young, Dye &amp; Wilkie, listed in schedules 6 and 6A.</td><td>Propagating material and fruit plants of the pre-basic category of <em>Prunus armeniaca</em> L.<em>, Prunus avium</em> L.<em>, Prunus cerasus</em> L., <em>Prunus domestica</em> L., and <em>Prunus dulcis</em> (Miller) Webb, must derive from mother plants, which have been tested within the previous growing season and found free from Plum pox virus.</td><td rowspan="2">In the case where a derogation is allowed to produce pre-basic material in the field under non-insect proof conditions, pursuant to Commission Implementing Decision 2017/925, the following requirements apply concerning <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider, Plum pox virus, <em>Xanthomonas arboricola</em> pv. <em>pruni</em> (Smith) Vauterin <em>et al.</em> and <em>Pseudomonas syringae</em> pv<em>. persicae</em> (Prunier, Luisetti &amp;. Gardan) Young, Dye &amp; Wilkie:</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="3">Pre-basic rootstocks of <em>Prunus cerasifera</em> Ehrh. and <em>Prunus domestica</em> L. must derive from mother plants, which have been tested within the previous growing season and found free from Plum pox virus. Pre-basic rootstocks of <em>Prunus cerasifera</em> Ehrh. and <em>Prunus domestica</em> L. must derive from mother plants, which have been tested within the previous five growing seasons and found free from <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(i) propagating material and fruit plants of the pre-basic category must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider,</td></tr>
  <tr><td>Each flowering pre-basic mother plant must be sampled and tested for Prune dwarf virus and <em>Prunus</em> necrotic ringspot virus one year after its acceptance as a pre-basic mother plant and with subsequent intervals of one year. In the case of <em>Prunus persica</em>, each flowering pre-basic mother plant must be sampled one year after its acceptance as a pre-basic mother plant and tested for Peach latent mosaic viroid. Each tree planted intentionally for pollination and, where appropriate, the major pollinating trees in the environment must be sampled and tested for Prune dwarf virus and <em>Prunus</em> necrotic ringspot virus.</td><td rowspan="2">(i) no symptoms of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider are observed on propagating material and fruit plants of the pre-basic category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed, or<br>(ii) propagating material and fruit plants of the pre-basic category in the production site must be isolated from other host plants. The isolation distance of the production site must depend on regional circumstances, the type of propagating material, the presence of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider in the area concerned and the relevant risks involved as set out by the competent authorities based on inspection,</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="10">Each pre-basic mother plant must be sampled five years after its acceptance as a pre-basic mother plant, and with subsequent intervals of five years, and tested for <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider and Plum pox virus. Each pre-basic mother plant must be sampled ten years after its acceptance as a pre-basic mother plant, and with subsequent intervals of ten years, and tested for RNQPs, other than Prune dwarf virus, Plum pox virus and <em>Prunus</em> necrotic ringspot virus, relevant for the species, as listed in schedule 6A, and tested where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedule 6. A representative portion of pre-basic mother plants must be sampled and tested where there are doubts concerning the presence of <em>Xanthomonas arboricola</em> pv. <em>pruni</em> (Smith) Vauterin <em>et al.</em></td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) Plum pox virus:</td></tr>
