  <tr><td>(i) propagating material and fruit plants of the pre-basic category must be produced in areas known to be free from Plum pox virus,<br>(ii) no symptoms of Plum pox virus are observed on propagating material and fruit plants of the pre-basic category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed, or<br>(iii) propagating material and fruit plants of the pre-basic category in the production site must be isolated from other host plants. The isolation distance of the production site must depend on regional circumstances, the type of propagating material, the presence of Plum pox virus in the area concerned and the relevant risks involved as set out by the competent authorities based on inspection,</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) <em>Pseudomonas syringae</em> pv<em>. persicae</em> (Prunier, Luisetti &amp;. Gardan) Young, Dye &amp; Wilkie:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(i) propagating material and fruit plants of the pre-basic category must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Pseudomonas syringae</em> pv<em>. persicae</em> (Prunier, Luisetti &amp;. Gardan) Young, Dye &amp; Wilkie, or<br>(ii) no symptoms of <em>Pseudomonas syringae</em> pv<em>. persicae</em> (Prunier, Luisetti &amp;. Gardan) Young, Dye &amp; Wilkie are observed on propagating material and fruit plants of the pre-basic category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed,</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(ca) Tomato ringspot virus:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(i) propagating material and fruit plants of the pre-basic category must be produced in areas known to be free from Tomato ringspot virus, or<br>(ii) no symptoms of Tomato ringspot virus are observed on propagating material and fruit plants of the pre-basic category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed,</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) <em>Xanthomonas arboricola</em> pv. pruni (Smith) Vauterin <em>et al</em>.:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(i) propagating material and fruit plants of the pre-basic category must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Xanthomonas arboricola</em> pv. <em>pruni</em> (Smith) Vauterin <em>et al</em>., or</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(i) no symptoms of <em>Xanthomonas arboricola</em> pv. <em>pruni</em> (Smith) Vauterin <em>et al</em>. are observed on propagating material and fruit plants of the pre-basic category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="14"><strong>Basic category</strong></td><td rowspan="38">Visual inspections must be carried out once a year.</td><td rowspan="2">A representative portion of basic mother plants that have been maintained in insect-proof facilities must be sampled every three years and tested concerning the presence of Prune dwarf virus, <em>Prunus</em> necrotic ringspot virus and Plum pox virus. A representative portion of basic mother plants must be sampled every ten years and tested concerning the presence of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider.</td><td><em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider:</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="6">(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the basic and certified categories must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider,<br>(b) no symptoms of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider are observed on propagating material and fruit plants of the basic and certified categories in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed, or<br>(c) symptoms of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider have been observed on no more than 1% of propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and that propagating material and those fruit plants, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed, and a representative sample of the remaining asymptomatic propagating material and fruit plants in the lots in which symptomatic plants were found has been tested and found free from <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>Mother plants which have not been maintained in insect proof facilities:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) a representative portion of basic mother plants, other than those intended for the production of rootstocks, must be sampled every year and tested for Plum pox virus in order to have all plants tested within an interval of ten years,</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) a representative portion of basic mother plants, intended for the production of rootstocks must be sampled every year and tested concerning the presence of Plum pox virus and found free from that RNQP. A representative portion of basic mother plants of <em>Prunus domestica</em> L. intended for the production of rootstocks must be sampled and tested in the previous five growing seasons concerning the presence of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider and found free from that RNQP, and</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(ba) a representative portion of basic mother plants must be sampled and tested every twenty years on the basis of an assessment of the risk of infection of those plants concerning the presence of Tomato ringspot virus, and</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) a representative portion of basic mother plants must be sampled and tested where there are doubts concerning the presence of <em>Xanthomonas arboricola</em> pv. <em>pruni</em> (Smith) Vauterin <em>et al.</em> A representative portion of basic mother plants must be sampled and tested every ten years on the basis of an assessment of the risk of infection of those plants concerning the presence of RNQPs, other than <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider, Prune dwarf virus, <em>Prunus</em> necrotic ringspot virus , Plum pox virus and Tomato ringspot virus, listed in schedule 6A, and tested where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedule 6.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="2">Flowering mother plants:</td><td>Plum pox virus:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the basic and certified categories must be produced in areas known to be free from Plum pox virus, or<br>(b) no symptoms of Plum pox virus are observed on propagating material and fruit plants of the basic and certified categories in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed, or<br>(c) symptoms of Plum pox virus have been observed on no more than 1% of propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and that propagating material and those fruit plants, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed, and a representative sample of the remaining asymptomatic propagating material and fruit plants in the lots in which symptomatic plants were found has been tested and found free from Plum pox virus.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) a representative portion of flowering basic mother plants must be sampled every year and tested for <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider, Prune dwarf virus and <em>Prunus</em> necrotic ringspot virus on the basis of an assessment of the risk of infection of those plants, and</td><td rowspan="3">Tomato ringspot virus:<br>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the basic category must be produced in areas known to be free from Tomato ringspot virus, or<br>(b) no symptoms of Tomato ringspot virus are observed on propagating material and fruit plants of the basic category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) in the case of <em>Prunus persica</em> (L.) Batsch, a representative portion of flowering basic mother plants must be sampled once a year and tested for Peach latent mosaic viroid on the basis of an assessment of the risk of infection of those plants. A representative portion of trees planted intentionally for pollination and, where appropriate, the major pollinating trees in the environment must be sampled and tested Prune dwarf virus and <em>Prunus</em> necrotic ringspot virus on the basis of an assessment of the risk of infection of those plants.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="3">A representative portion of non-flowering basic mother plants which have been not maintained in insect proof facilities must be sampled and tested every three years concerning the presence of Prune dwarf virus, <em>Prunus</em> necrotic ringspot virus and <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider on the basis of an assessment of the risk of infection of those plants.</td></tr>
  <tr><td><em>Pseudomonas syringae</em> pv<em>. persicae</em> (Prunier, Luisetti &amp;. Gardan) Young, Dye &amp; Wilkie:</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="2">(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the basic and certified categories must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Pseudomonas syringae</em> pv. <em>persicae</em> (Prunier, Luisetti &amp;. Gardan) Young, Dye &amp; Wilkie,<br>(b) no symptoms of <em>Pseudomonas syringae</em> pv<em>. persicae</em> (Prunier, Luisetti &amp;. Gardan) Young, Dye &amp; Wilkie are observed on propagating material and fruit plants of the basic and certified categories in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed, or<br>(c) symptoms of <em>Pseudomonas syringae</em> pv<em>. persicae</em> (Prunier, Luisetti &amp;. Gardan) Young, Dye &amp; Wilkie have been observed on no more than 2% of propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and that propagating material and those fruit plants, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="9"><strong>Certified category</strong></td><td>A representative portion of certified mother plants that have been maintained in insect proof facilities must be sampled every five years and tested concerning the presence of Prune dwarf virus, <em>Prunus</em> necrotic ringspot virus and Plum pox virus in order to have all plants tested within an interval of fifteen years. A representative portion of certified mother plants must be sampled every fifteen years and tested concerning the presence of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="2">Mother plants that have not been maintained in insect proof facilities:</td><td>Tomato ringspot virus:<br>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category must be produced in areas known to be free from Tomato ringspot virus; or<br>(b) no symptoms of Tomato ringspot virus are observed on propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td><em>Xanthomonas arboricola</em> pv. <em>pruni</em> (Smith) Vauterin et al.:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) a representative portion of certified mother plants that have not been maintained in insect proof facilities must be sampled every three years and tested for Plum pox virus in order to have all plants tested within an interval of fifteen years,</td><td rowspan="6">(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the basic and certified categories must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Xanthomonas arboricola</em> pv. <em>pruni</em> (Smith) Vauterin <em>et al.</em>,<br>(b) no symptoms of <em>Xanthomonas arboricola</em> pv. <em>pruni</em> (Smith) Vauterin <em>et al.</em> are observed on propagating material and fruit plants of the basic and certified categories in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed; or<br>(c) symptoms of <em>Xanthomonas arboricola</em> pv. <em>pruni</em> (Smith) Vauterin <em>et al.</em> have been observed on no more than 2% of propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and that propagating material and those fruit plants, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) a representative portion of certified mother plants intended for the production of rootstocks must be sampled every year and tested concerning the presence of Plum pox virus and found free from that RNQP. A representative portion of certified mother plants of <em>Prunus cerasifera</em> Ehrh. and <em>Prunus domestica</em> L. intended for the production of rootstocks have been sampled in the previous five growing seasons and tested concerning the presence of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider and found free from that RNQP, ...</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(ba) a representative portion of certified mother plants shall be sampled every twenty years and tested on the basis of an assessment of the risk of infection of those plants concerning the presence of Tomato ringspot virus, and</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) a representative portion of certified mother plants must be sampled and tested where there are doubts concerning the presence of <em>Xanthomonas arboricola</em> pv. <em>pruni</em> (Smith) Vauterin <em>et al.</em> A representative portion of certified mother plants must be sampled every fifteen years and tested on the basis of an assessment of the risk of infection of those plants concerning the presence of RNQPs, other than <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider, Prune dwarf virus, <em>Prunus</em> necrotic ringspot virus , Plum pox virus and Tomato ringspot virus, listed in schedule 6A, and tested where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedule 6.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>A representative portion of flowering certified mother plants must be sampled every year and tested for <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider, Prune dwarf virus and <em>Prunus</em> necrotic ringspot virus on the basis of an assessment of the risk of infection of those plants. In the case of <em>Prunus persica</em> (L.) Batsch, a representative portion of flowering certified mother plants must be sampled once a year and tested for Peach latent mosaic viroid on the basis of an assessment of the risk of infection of those plants. A representative portion of trees planted intentionally for pollination and, where appropriate, the major pollinating trees in the environment must be sampled and tested for Prune dwarf virus and <em>Prunus</em> necrotic ringspot virus on the basis of an assessment of the risk of infection of those plants.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>A representative portion of non-flowering certified mother plants, which have not been maintained in insect proof facilities, must be sampled every three years and tested concerning the presence of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum, Prune dwarf virus and <em>Prunus</em> necrotic ringspot virus on the basis of an assessment of the risk of infection of those plants.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="15"><strong>CAC category</strong></td><td rowspan="2">Propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category must derive from an identified source of material, of which a representative portion has been sampled and tested within the previous three growing seasons and found free from Plum pox virus.</td><td><em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider:</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="6">(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider,<br>(b) no symptoms of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider are observed on propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed,<br>(c) symptoms of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider have been observed on no more than 1% of propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and that propagating material and those fruit plants, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed, and a representative sample of the remaining asymptomatic propagating material and fruit plants in the lots in which symptomatic propagating material and fruit plants were found has been tested and found free from <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider; or<br>(d) ...  </td></tr>
  <tr><td>CAC rootstocks of <em>Prunus cerasifera</em> Ehrh. and <em>Prunus domestica</em> L. must derive from an identified source of material of which a representative portion has been sampled and tested within the previous 5 years and found free from <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider and Plum pox virus.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>A representative portion of propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category must be sampled and tested where there are doubts concerning the presence of <em>Xanthomonas arboricola</em> pv. <em>pruni</em> (Smith) Vauterin <em>et al</em>.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>A representative portion of CAC fruit plants not showing any symptoms of Plum pox virus upon visual inspection must be sampled and tested on the basis of an assessment of the risk of infection of those fruit plants concerning the presence of that RNQP and in the case of symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>Upon the detection of propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category showing symptoms of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider in the production site by visual inspection, a representative portion of the remaining asymptomatic CAC propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category in the lots where symptomatic propagating material and fruit plants have been found must be sampled and tested concerning the presence of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="9">Sampling and testing must be carried out where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs, other than <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma prunorum Seemüller &amp; Schneider and Plum pox virus, listed in schedules 6 and 6A.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>Plum pox virus:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category must be produced in areas known to be free from Plum pox virus,<br>(b) no symptoms of Plum pox virus are observed on propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed, or</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) symptoms of Plum pox virus have been observed on no more than 1% of propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and that propagating material and those fruit plants, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed, and a representative sample of the remaining asymptomatic propagating material and fruit plants in the lots in which symptomatic propagating material and fruit plants were found has been tested and found free from Plum pox virus.</td></tr>
  <tr><td><em>Pseudomonas syringae</em> pv. <em>persicae</em> (Prunier, Luisetti &amp;. Gardan) Young, Dye &amp; Wilkie:<br>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Pseudomonas syringae</em> pv. <em>persicae</em> (Prunier, Luisetti &amp;. Gardan) Young, Dye &amp; Wilkie,<br>(b) no symptoms of <em>Pseudomonas syringae</em> pv. <em>persicae</em> (Prunier, Luisetti &amp;. Gardan) Young, Dye &amp; Wilkie are observed on propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed, or<br>(c) symptoms of <em>Pseudomonas syringae</em> pv. <em>persicae</em> (Prunier, Luisetti &amp;. Gardan) Young, Dye &amp; Wilkie have been observed on no more than 2% of propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and that propagating material and those fruit plants, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>Tomato ringspot virus:<br>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category must be produced in areas known to be free from Tomato ringspot virus,<br>(b) no symptoms of Tomato ringspot virus are observed in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed, or<br>(c) symptoms of Tomato ringspot virus have been observed on no more than 2% of propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and those symptomatic plants have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td><em>Xanthomonas arboricola</em> pv. pruni (Smith) Vauterin <em>et al</em>.:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Xanthomonas arboricola</em> pv. pruni (Smith) Vauterin <em>et al</em>.,</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) no symptoms of <em>Xanthomonas arboricola</em> pv. <em>pruni</em> (Smith) Vauterin <em>et al.</em> are observed on propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed, or<br>(b) symptoms of <em>Xanthomonas arboricola</em> pv. <em>pruni</em> (Smith) Vauterin <em>et al.</em> have been observed on no more than 2% of propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and that propagating material and those fruit plants, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="19"><em><strong>Pyrus</strong></em> <strong>L.</strong></td><td rowspan="7"><strong>Pre-basic category</strong></td><td rowspan="19">Visual inspections must be carried out once a year.</td><td rowspan="7">Each pre-basic mother plant must be sampled and tested fifteen years after its acceptance as a pre-basic mother plant and with subsequent intervals of fifteen years concerning the presence of RNQPs other than virus-like diseases and viroids listed in schedule 6A, and where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedule 6.</td><td>In the case where a derogation is allowed to produce pre-basic material in the field under non-insect proof conditions, pursuant to Commission Implementing Decision 2017/925, the following requirements apply concerning <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma pyri Seemüller &amp; Schneider and E<em>rwinia amylovora</em> (Burrill) Winslow <em>et al.</em>:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma pyri Seemüller &amp; Schneider:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(i) propagating material and fruit plants of the pre-basic category must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma pyri Seemüller &amp; Schneider, or</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(i) no symptoms of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma pyri Seemüller &amp; Schneider are observed on propagating material and fruit plants of the pre-basic category at the production site during the last three growing seasons, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed,</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) <em>Erwinia amylovora</em> (Burrill) Winslow et al.:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(i) propagating material and fruit plants of the pre-basic category must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Erwinia amylovora</em> (Burrill) Winslow <em>et al.</em>, or</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(i) propagating material and fruit plants of the pre-basic category in the production site have been inspected over the last complete growing season, and any propagating material and fruit plants showing symptoms of <em>Erwinia amylovora</em> (Burrill) Winslow <em>et al.</em> and any surrounding host plants have been immediately rogued out and destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="3"><strong>Basic category</strong></td><td rowspan="2">In the case of basic mother plants which have been maintained in insect proof facilities, a representative portion of basic mother plants must be sampled and tested every fifteen years concerning the presence of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma pyri Seemüller &amp; Schneider.</td><td><em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma pyri Seemüller &amp; Schneider:</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="4">(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the basic and certified categories must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma pyri Seemüller &amp; Schneider,<br>(b) no symptoms of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma pyri Seemüller &amp; Schneider are observed on propagating material and fruit plants of the basic and certified categories at the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed, or<br>(c) propagating material and fruit plants of the basic and certified categories at the production site and any plants in the immediate vicinity, which have shown symptoms of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma pyri Seemüller &amp; Schneider during visual inspections during the last three growing seasons, must be rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>In the case of basic mother plants which have been not maintained in insect proof facilities, a representative portion of basic mother plants must be sampled and tested every three years concerning the presence of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma pyri Seemüller &amp; Schneider; a representative portion of basic mother plants must be sampled and tested every fifteen years on the basis of an assessment of the risk of infection of those plants concerning the presence of RNQPs, other than <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma pyri Seemüller &amp; Schneider and other than the virus-like diseases and viroids, listed in schedule 6A, and where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedule 6.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="5"><strong>Certified category</strong></td><td>In the case of certified mother plants, which have been maintained in insect proof facilities, a representative portion of certified mother plants must be sampled and tested every fifteen years concerning the presence of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma pyri Seemüller &amp; Schneider.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="3">In the case of certified mother plants, which have been not maintained in insect proof facilities, a representative portion of certified mother plants must be sampled and tested every five years concerning the presence of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma pyri Seemüller &amp; Schneider; a representative portion of certified mother plants must be sampled and tested every fifteen years on the basis of an assessment of the risk of infection of those plants concerning the presence of RNQPs, other than <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma pyri Seemüller &amp; Schneider and other than virus-like diseases and viroids, listed in schedule 6A, and where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedule 6.</td></tr>
  <tr><td><em>Erwinia amylovora</em> (Burrill) Winslow et al.:</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="2">(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the basic and certified categories must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Erwinia amylovora</em> (Burrill) Winslow et al., or<br>(b) propagating material and fruit plants of the basic and certified categories in the production site have been inspected over the last complete growing season, and any propagating material and fruit plants showing symptoms of <em>Erwinia amylovora</em> (Burrill) Winslow <em>et al.</em> and any surrounding host plants have been immediately rogued out and destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>Certified fruit plants must be sampled and tested where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedules 6 and 6A.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="4"><strong>CAC category</strong></td><td rowspan="4">Sampling and testing must be carried out where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedules 6 and 6A.</td><td><em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma pyri Seemuller &amp; Schneider:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma pyri Seemüller &amp; Schneider,<br>(b) no symptoms of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma pyri Seemüller &amp; Schneider are observed on propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category at the production site over the last complete growing season, and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed, or<br>(c) propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category at the production site and any plants in the immediate vicinity, which have shown symptoms of <em>Candidatus</em> Phytoplasma pyri Seemüller &amp; Schneider during visual inspections during the last three growing seasons, have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td><em>Erwinia amylovora</em> (Burrill) Winslow <em>et al</em>.:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Erwinia amylovora</em> (Burrill) Winslow <em>et al.</em>, or<br>(b) propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category in the production site have been inspected over the last complete growing season, and any propagating material and fruit plants showing symptoms of <em>Erwinia amylovora</em> (Burrill) Winslow <em>et al.</em> and any surrounding host plants have been immediately rogued out and destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="4"><em><strong>Ribes</strong></em> <strong>L.</strong></td><td><strong>Pre-basic category</strong></td><td>Visual inspections must be carried out twice a year.</td><td>Each pre-basic mother plant must be sampled and tested four years after its acceptance as a pre-basic mother plant and with subsequent intervals of four years concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedule 6A, and where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedule 6.</td><td>N/A</td></tr>
  <tr><td><strong>Basic category</strong></td><td rowspan="3">Visual inspections must be carried out once a year.</td><td rowspan="3">Sampling and testing must be carried out where there are doubts concerning the presence of the RNQPs listed in schedules 6 and 6A.</td><td>The percentage of propagating material and fruit plants of the basic category in the production site over the last complete growing season showing symptoms of <em>Aphelenchoides ritzemabosi</em> (Schwartz) Steiner &amp; Buhrer must not exceed 0.05% and that propagating material and those fruit plants, and any surrounding host plants have been rogued out and destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td><strong>Certified category</strong></td><td>The percentage of propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category in the production site over the last complete growing season showing symptoms of <em>Aphelenchoides ritzemabosi</em> (Schwartz) Steiner &amp; Buhrer must not exceed 0.5% and that propagating material and those fruit plants, and any surrounding host plants have been rogued out and destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td><strong>CAC category</strong></td><td>N/A</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="14"><em><strong>Rubus</strong></em> <strong>L.</strong></td><td><strong>Pre-basic category</strong></td><td>Visual inspections must be carried out twice a year.</td><td>Each pre-basic mother plant must be sampled and tested two years after its acceptance as a pre-basic mother plant and with subsequent intervals of two years concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedule 6A, and where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedule 6.</td><td>N/A.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="5"><strong>Basic category</strong></td><td>Where propagating material and fruit plants are grown in the field or in pots, visual inspections must be carried out twice a year.</td><td rowspan="5">Sampling and testing must be carried out if the symptoms of <em>Arabis</em> mosaic virus, Raspberry ringspot virus, Strawberry latent ringspot virus , Tomato black ring virus and Tomato ringspot virus are unclear upon visual inspection. Sampling and testing must be carried out where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs, other than <em>Arabis</em> mosaic virus, Raspberry ringspot virus, Strawberry latent ringspot virus , Tomato black ring virus and Tomato ringspot virus, listed in schedules 6 and 6A</td><td rowspan="2">In the case of a positive test result for propagating material and fruit plants of the basic category showing symptoms of <em>Arabis</em> mosaic virus, Raspberry ringspot virus, Strawberry latent ringspot virus , Tomato black ring virus or Tomato ringspot virus, the propagating material and fruit plants concerned must be rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="4">For propagating material and fruit plants produced by micropropagation, and which are maintained for a period shorter than three months, only one visual inspection during this period is necessary.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>In relation to RNQPs other than <em>Arabis</em> mosaic virus, Raspberry ringspot virus, Strawberry latent ringspot virus , Tomato black ring virus and Tomato ringspot virus, the percentage of propagating material and fruit plants of the basic category in the production site over the last complete growing season, showing symptoms of each of the following RNQPs must not exceed 0.1% in the case of:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) <em>Agrobacterium</em> spp. Conn.; and<br>(b) <em>Rhodococcus fascians</em> Tilford; and that propagating material and those fruit plants, and any surrounding host plants have been rogued out and destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>Symptoms of all viruses listed in schedules 6 and 6A have been observed on no more than 0.25% of propagating material and fruit plants of the basic category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and that propagating material and those fruit plants and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="6"><strong>Certified category</strong></td><td rowspan="6">Visual inspections must be carried out once a year.</td><td rowspan="6">Sampling and testing must be carried out if the symptoms of <em>Arabis</em> mosaic virus, Raspberry ringspot virus, Strawberry latent ringspot virus , Tomato black ring virus and Tomato ringspot virus are unclear upon visual inspection. Sampling and testing must be carried out where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs, other than <em>Arabis</em> mosaic virus, Raspberry ringspot virus, Strawberry latent ringspot virus , Tomato black ring virus and Tomato ringspot virus, listed in schedules 6 and 6A.</td><td>In the case of a positive test result for propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category showing symptoms of <em>Arabis</em> mosaic virus, Raspberry ringspot virus, Strawberry latent ringspot virus , Tomato black ring virus or Tomato ringspot virus, the propagating material and fruit plants concerned must be rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>In relation to RNQPs other than <em>Arabis</em> mosaic virus, Raspberry ringspot virus, Strawberry latent ringspot virus , Tomato black ring virus and Tomato ringspot virus, the percentage of propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category in the production site over the last complete growing season, showing symptoms of each of the following RNQPs must not exceed:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) 0.5% in the case of <em>Resseliella theobaldi</em> Barnes, and<br>(b) 1% in the case of:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(i) <em>Agrobacterium</em> spp. Conn., and<br>(ii) Rhodococcus fascians Tilford,</td></tr>
  <tr><td>and that propagating material and those fruit plants, and any surrounding host plants have been rogued out and destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>Symptoms of all viruses listed in schedules 6 and 6A have been observed on no more than 0.5% of propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and that propagating material and those fruit plants and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="2"><strong>CAC category</strong></td><td rowspan="2">Visual inspections must be carried out once a year.</td><td rowspan="2">Sampling and testing must be carried out if the symptoms of <em>Arabis</em> mosaic virus, Raspberry ringspot virus, Strawberry latent ringspot virus , Tomato black ring virus and Tomato ringspot virus are unclear upon visual inspection. Sampling and testing must be carried out where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs, other than <em>Arabis</em> mosaic virus, Raspberry ringspot virus, Strawberry latent ringspot virus , Tomato black ring virus and Tomato ringspot virus, listed in schedules 6 and 6A.</td><td>In the case of a positive test result for propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category showing symptoms of <em>Arabis</em> mosaic virus, Raspberry ringspot virus, Strawberry latent ringspot virus , Tomato black ring virus or Tomato ringspot virus, the propagating material and fruit plants concerned must be rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>Symptoms of tomato ringspot virus have been observed on no more than 2% of propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and those symptomatic plants have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="16"><em><strong>Vaccinium</strong></em> <strong>L.</strong></td><td><strong>Pre-basic category</strong></td><td>Visual inspections must be carried out twice a year.</td><td>Each pre-basic mother plant must be sampled and tested five years after its acceptance as a pre-basic mother plant and with subsequent intervals of five years concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedule 6A, and where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedule 6.</td><td>N/A.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="7"><strong>Basic category</strong></td><td rowspan="7">Visual inspections must be carried out twice a year.</td><td rowspan="7">Sampling and testing must be carried out where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedules 6 and 6A.</td><td>No symptoms of <em>Agrobacterium tumefaciens</em> (Smith &amp; Townsend) Conn are observed at the production site over the last complete growing season.</td></tr>
  <tr><td><em>Diaporthe vaccinii</em> Shear:<br>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the basic category must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Diaporthe vaccinii</em> Shear, or<br>(b) no symptoms of <em>Diaporthe vaccinii</em> Shear are observed at the production site over the last complete growing season.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>Tobacco ringspot virus:<br>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the basic category must be produced in areas known to be free from Tobacco ringspot virus, or<br>(b) no symptoms of Tobacco ringspot virus are observed in the production site over the last complete growing season and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>Tomato ringspot virus:<br>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the basic category must be produced in areas known to be free from Tomato ringspot virus, or<br>(b) no symptoms of Tomato ringspot virus are observed in the production site over the last complete growing season and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>In relation to <em>Exobasidium vaccinii (</em>Fuckel) Woronin and <em>Godronia cassandrae</em> (anamorph <em>Topospora myrtilli</em>) Peck, the percentage of propagating material and fruit plants of the basic category in the production site over the last complete growing season, showing symptoms of each of the following RNQPs must not exceed:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) 0.1% in the case of <em>Godronia cassandrae</em> (anamorph <em>Topospora myrtilli</em>) Peck, and<br>(b) 0.5% in the case of <em>Exobasidium vaccinii</em> (Fuckel) Woronin,</td></tr>
  <tr><td>and that propagating material and those fruit plants, and any surrounding host plants have been rogued out and destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="8"><strong>Certified category</strong></td><td rowspan="10">Visual inspections must be carried out once a year.</td><td rowspan="10">Sampling and testing must be carried out where there are doubts concerning the presence of RNQPs listed in schedules 6 and 6A.</td><td><em>Diaporthe vaccinii</em> Shear:<br>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category must be produced in areas known to be free from <em>Diaporthe vaccinii</em> Shear, or<br>(b) no symptoms of <em>Diaporthe vaccinii</em> Shear are observed at the production site over the last complete growing season.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>Tobacco ringspot virus:<br>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category must be produced in areas known to be free from Tobacco ringspot virus, or<br>(b) no symptoms of Tobacco ringspot virus have been observed in the production site over the last complete growing season and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>Tomato ringspot virus:<br>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category must be produced in areas known to be free from Tomato ringspot virus, or<br>(b) no symptoms of Tomato ringspot virus have been observed in the production site over the last complete growing season and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>In relation to <em>Agrobacterium tumefaciens</em> (Smith &amp; Townsend) Conn, <em>Exobasidium vaccinii</em> (Fuckel) Woronin and <em>Godronia cassandrae</em> (anamorph <em>Topospora myrtilli</em>) Peck, the percentage of propagating material and fruit plants of the certified category in the production site over the last complete growing season, showing symptoms of each of the following RNQPs must not exceed:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) 0.5% in the case of:</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(i) <em>Agrobacterium tumefaciens</em> (Smith &amp; Townsend) Conn, and<br>(ii) <em>Godronia cassandrae</em> (anamorph <em>Topospora myrtilli</em>) Peck, and</td></tr>
  <tr><td>(a) 1% in the case of <em>Exobasidium vaccinii</em> (Fuckel) Woronin;</td></tr>
  <tr><td>and that propagating material and those fruit plants, and any surrounding host plants have been rogued out and destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td rowspan="2"></td><td rowspan="2"><strong>CAC category</strong></td><td>Tobacco ringspot virus:<br>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category must be produced in areas known to be free from Tobacco ringspot virus,<br>(b) no symptoms of Tobacco ringspot virus are observed in the production site over the last complete growing season and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed, or<br>(c) symptoms of Tobacco ringspot virus have been observed on no more than 2% of propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and those symptomatic plants have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>Tomato ringspot virus:<br>(a) propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category must be produced in areas known to be free from Tomato ringspot virus,<br>(b) no symptoms of Tomato ringspot virus are observed in the production site over the last complete growing season and any symptomatic plants in the immediate vicinity have been rogued out and immediately destroyed, or<br>(c) symptoms of Tomato ringspot virus have been observed on no more than 2% of propagating material and fruit plants of the CAC category in the production site over the last complete growing season, and those symptomatic plants have been rogued out and immediately destroyed.</td></tr>
  </tbody>

  [^c-3aea41be428dc406d7d1db8fb53b531c][^c-56351badf8367239ddb52972a8cf2ad9][^c-4d6ed8ad5cfd025ae35ee4dd3e2822be][^c-43e05b665813b0640708480e3063f9db][^c-f2eaa4bad6cd077fc1273744257ad438][^c-c59f6c6fa79f8fe52cc8aa8211beb2b2][^c-bf0258e862ef772f5406081ce922daec][^c-445414dc4541d5d647106875740d4262][^c-67d6728e49a7e911c2519fa7106a9c44][^c-40c1033b49e77f6f0be4a375c4d285c4][^c-1d3d5652e7d76476ce41daa91ca82914][^c-a0d2f35e4c58421fe270174c2a88958b][^c-347005449ba940b5f7661c0533224b8b][^c-3615429e533f5c957323188500c1b8aa][^c-07aadb910921aca4bd2c1729bd891f6b][^c-6473e2aba59ca5d225a021b870491805][^c-d9290ef90f66c5df35d94d63f7c1bbc9][^c-2e98835980b9a1e98dfe3b587deb50ae][^c-e43b68fc7158c1f5ce4524953814cbf7][^c-f08306564025af21f2ffa59febb6d2d5][^c-051f45bb545d8af4c6f244ba11a9cbbf][^c-be0ae70f5a1247beaa09ac3eee65d92a][^c-8a16be2ce4303e82abc7a53a6686ed37][^c-e9fc683da8b3077d97c3233d91e1a76d][^c-20a73420775d8a587b0af39cd5a45b80][^c-9cf684d415897a27fd4b011777ba3da7][^c-20187c190e7a52de65e31b6d6c028831][^c-fa9deaa759f5b7cea6fd0175ab7a360a][^c-ba51e57fe55aaafb49b65ef49a4d79f9][^c-e54262a0846bc1bcd239d4e770eb1425][^c-67d88679b1e4c5c2051b6f84f18701b0][^c-f0cdd35769d82aaea2f9f86759d7cf06][^c-25ec8c85595a524a43fcb3d1dcbbe550][^c-228cb3f87e193178a0a11cedd1421abd]

## SCHEDULE 8 — Powers of inspectors

Regulation 18(2)

### 1. Powers of entry etc.

(1) An inspector may, on producing a duly authenticated authorisation if so required, enter any premises in Scotland of a supplier, at any reasonable time, if the inspector reasonably suspects that any activity to which these Regulations apply is being carried out on those premises, for the purposes of ascertaining whether there is, or has been, any contravention of these Regulations.

(2) An inspector entering premises under sub-paragraph (1) may—

- (a)[^c-1b94081c7eb7df7cfaeb7e3403e85930] be accompanied by such other persons as the inspector considers appropriate;
- (b) take onto those premises any equipment or materials that the inspector considers necessary for the enforcement of these Regulations;
- (c) open any container;
- (d) carry out any searches, inspections, measurements and tests;
- (e) take samples;
- (f) have access to, and inspect, any books, documents or records (in whatever form they are held) relating to these Regulations and remove them to enable them to be copied;
- (g) photograph or copy anything, the production of which the inspector has the power to require under head (f);
- (h) photograph anything which the inspector has reasonable cause to believe may be relevant in connection with the enforcement of these Regulations; and
- (i) seize any computers and associated equipment for the purpose of copying documents, provided that they are returned as soon as practicable.

(3) Any person who accompanies an inspector in accordance with this paragraph may perform any of the inspector's functions but only under the supervision of that inspector.

### 2. Information notice

An inspector may, by notice served on any person, require that person to provide such information as is specified in the notice in such form and within such period following service of the notice or at such time as is so specified (in this schedule, “an information notice”).

### 3. Prohibition on movement

An inspector may, by notice served on any person, prohibit that person from moving plant material from any premises if the inspector has reasonable grounds to suspect that the plant material fails to comply with a requirement to which it is subject by virtue of schedule 2, 3 or 5 (in this schedule, “a movement notice”).

### 4. Enforcement and prohibition notices

(1) An inspector may serve a notice on any person who contravenes, or who the inspector has reasonable grounds to suspect may contravene, these Regulations—

- (a) requiring that person to act in accordance with the Regulations (in this schedule, an “enforcement notice”); or
- (b) prohibiting that person from acting in breach of the Regulations (in this schedule, a “prohibition notice”).

(2) The notice must give reasons for serving it and, if appropriate, specify what action must be taken and specify the time limit for taking any such action.

### 5. Appeals against enforcement and prohibition notices

(1) Any person who is aggrieved by a decision of an inspector to serve a notice under this schedule may appeal by application to the sheriff.

(2) The period within which an appeal must be brought is 28 days from the service of the notice or, in the case of an enforcement notice, the period specified in the notice, whichever ends earlier.

(3) A notice served under this schedule must state—

- (a) the right of appeal to the sheriff by the person on whom the notice is served; and
- (b) the period in which such an appeal may be brought.

(4) On an appeal under this paragraph, the sheriff may either cancel or affirm the notice and, if the sheriff affirms the notice, the sheriff may do so either in its original form or with such modifications as the sheriff thinks fit.

### 6. Compliance with notices

(1) A notice served under this schedule must be complied with at the expense of the person on whom it is served and, if it is not complied with, an inspector may make arrangements to secure compliance with the notice.

(2) Where an inspector makes arrangements pursuant to sub-paragraph (1), the Scottish Ministers may recover all reasonable expenses in connection with the arrangements as a debt from the person on whom the notice was served.

*(This note is not part of the Regulations)*

These Regulations, which apply in Scotland, continue to implement Council Directive [2008/90/EC](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/european/directive/2008/0090) on the marketing of fruit plant propagating material and fruit plants intended for fruit production (OJ No L 267, 8.10.2008, p.8). They also implement—

- Commission Implementing Directive 2014/96/EU on the requirements for the labelling, sealing and packaging of fruit plant propagating material and fruit plants intended for fruit production, falling within the scope of Council Directive [2008/90/EC](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/european/directive/2008/0090) (OJ L 298, 16.10.2014, p.12);
- Commission Implementing Directive 2014/97/EU implementing Council Directive [2008/90/EC](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/european/directive/2008/0090) as regards the registration of suppliers and of varieties and the common list of varieties (OJ L 298, 16.10.2014, p.16);
- Commission Implementing Directive 2014/98/EU implementing Council Directive [2008/90/EC](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/european/directive/2008/0090) as regards specific requirements for the genus and species of fruit plants referred to in Annex I thereto, specific requirements to be met by suppliers and detailed rules concerning official inspections (OJ No L, 298, 16.10.2014, p.22).

They revoke and replace the Marketing of Fruit Plant Material Regulations (S.I. 2010/2079) as regards Scotland (see regulation 24).

Part 1 of the Regulations is introductory. The fruit plants and propagating material (plant material) to which the Regulations apply is set out in regulation 3 and schedule 1.

Part 2 contains requirements for marketing plant material (regulation 5). In order to be marketed, plant material must comply with requirements set out for certification (regulation 5(1) and schedule 2) and packaging, sealing and labelling (regulation 8 and schedule 5). Plant material must be, or be in the process of being, registered (schedule 4) and have been granted, or an application made for, plant variety rights (regulation 7). CAC material must comply with requirements set out in schedule 3 and be accompanied by a supplier's document (schedule 5, part 2). Regulation 6 sets out exceptions to these general requirements.

Part 3 requires the registration of suppliers (regulation 10) and a register of suppliers (regulation 11). Suppliers are required to monitor the production of plant material (regulation 14), remove non-compliant materials (regulation 15), separate plant materials (regulation 16) and keep records (regulation 17).

Part 4 deals with the enforcement and administration of these Regulations and sets out inspectors' (appointed by the Scottish Ministers) powers, including a power to serve a notice on any person to require that person to provide information and a power to prohibit the movement of plant material suspected of failing to comply with these Regulations (schedule 8). An inspector also has a power to serve a notice on any person acting in contravention of these Regulations to require that person to comply with the Regulations or to prohibit that person from acting in breach of them. Under regulation 20(1) a person who fails to comply with any such notice or to give assistance to the inspector commits an offence. Under regulation 20(2) a person who commits an offence under the Regulations is liable on summary conviction to a fine not exceeding level 3 on the standard scale. Regulation 22 makes provision for arrangements for official measures.

Part 5 sets out a transitional provision (regulation 23) and a revocation provision (regulation 24).

No business and regulatory impact assessment has been prepared for these Regulations as no impact or no significant impact upon business, charities or voluntary bodies is foreseen.
